# Digest 20261003-034953

Date: 2026-10-03. **Backfill run for 2024** (`since: 2024-01-01`, `until: 2024-12-31`), requested by the user, limited to the EGFR-focused searches: egfr-nsclc, nsclc-resistance-nextgen, lung-top-journals. The broad searches (lung-key-evidence, lung-nonpharm), the preprint feeds (which cannot be backfilled) and approvals-access were not run. The regular date window is unaffected (`--until` never moves it).

## Source errors and gaps

- **No source errors and no truncation.** Long PubMed windows were split into date slices (`slices` in `state/runs.jsonl`) and every match was retrieved.
- **Lighter backfill triage** (CLAUDE.md, step 4): every item has a relevance, but low items have no reason and medium reasons are one line. Triage was done in parallel batches from titles, publication types and abstracts or registry fields; where an abstract was cut off or missing, the entry says so.
- The PubMed window uses the Entrez date (when PubMed added the record), so some items show a 2025 publication year.
- ClinicalTrials.gov "results posted" items carry no outcome numbers in the monitor record; read the posted results on the registry before citing findings.
- Many 2024 results have since been updated (e.g. MARIPOSA, FLAURA2 and LAURA survival data in the 2025 and 2026 digests).

## High

### egfr-nsclc

- **Overall Survival From the EORTC LCG-1613 APPLE Trial of Osimertinib Versus Gefitinib Followed by Osimertinib in Advanced EGFR-Mutant Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.**: Randomised phase II APPLE trial (n=156, treatment-naive) comparing upfront osimertinib with gefitinib followed by osimertinib at plasma T790M or radiologic progression. Upfront osimertinib lowered the risk of brain progression (HR 0.54, 90% CI 0.34-0.86) but OS was comparable (HR 1.01, 90% CI 0.61-1.68; 18-month OS 84% vs 82.3%). This is direct sequencing evidence on the first-line choice; limitations are the phase II size, wide CIs and a baseline imbalance in brain metastases (29% vs 19% in the sequential arm).
  Remon J, Besse B, Aix SP et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38324744/
- **Nivolumab Plus Chemotherapy in Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Mutated Metastatic Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer After Disease Progression on Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors: Final Results of CheckMate 722.**: Randomised phase III trial (n=294) of nivolumab plus platinum-doublet vs platinum-doublet alone after progression on EGFR TKIs (1st/2nd-gen without T790M, or osimertinib). At final analysis (median follow-up 38.1 months) PFS was not significantly improved (median 5.6 vs 5.4 months; HR 0.75, 95% CI 0.56-1.00; P=.0528); median OS 19.4 vs 15.9 months, ORR 31.3% vs 26.7%, with more grade 3/4 TRAEs (44.7% vs 29.4%). Adds to the negative evidence for PD-1 blockade after EGFR-TKI failure; the favourable subgroups (sensitising mutation, one prior TKI) were post hoc.
  Mok T, Nakagawa K, Park K et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38252907/
- **First-in-human phase I study of BEBT-109 in previously treated EGFR exon 20 insertion-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer.**: Two-stage first-in-human study of the oral pan-mutant-selective EGFR inhibitor BEBT-109: dose escalation in 11 T790M patients (20-180 mg/d, no DLT or MTD reached) and dose expansion in 18 patients with treatment-refractory exon 20 insertion NSCLC. In the expansion cohort ORR was 44.4% (8/18) and median PFS 8.0 months (95% CI 1.33-14.67); diarrhoea occurred in all patients (22.2% grade >=3). Early signal for a new oral exon 20 insertion agent, but based on 18 patients, single-arm, with wide CIs.
  Zeng L, Song L, Liu L et al. *Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38521070/
- **Executive summary of the American Radium Society appropriate use criteria for brain metastases in epidermal growth factor receptor mutated-mutated and ALK-fusion non-small cell lung cancer.**: Guideline panel (ARS CNS committee) using systematic literature review (searches 2019 and 2023) and the UCLA/RAND appropriateness method across 8 model cases on whether radiotherapy can be deferred with CNS-penetrant EGFR/ALK TKIs and whether TKIs should be given concurrently with RT. Consensus was reached in only 4 scenarios; the panel did not reach consensus that RT can be deferred, although median scores suggested deferral may be appropriate in most situations, and WBRT with concurrent TKI was judged usually not appropriate for 2-4 brain metastases. Directly relevant to CNS management in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, but the panel found no definitive studies on TKI/RT sequencing, so recommendations rest on expert voting.
  Nagpal S, Milano MT, Chiang VL et al. *Neuro Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38459978/
- **Discovery of a Novel Potent EGFR Inhibitor Against EGFR Activating Mutations and On-Target Resistance in NSCLC.**: Preclinical evaluation of BI-4732, a fourth-generation EGFR TKI, in Ba/F3 cells and patient-derived cell, organoid and xenograft models, including an intracranial mouse model. BI-4732 showed marked single-agent activity in models with C797S-mediated osimertinib resistance, activity comparable to osimertinib against E19del/L858R/T790M, synergy with osimertinib, and intracranial activity with low blood-brain-barrier efflux. It is a candidate post-osimertinib on-target option; all data are preclinical, with no clinical results yet.
  Lee EJ, Oh SY, Lee YW et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38330145/
- **Functional Heterogeneity in MET Pathway Activation in PDX Models of Osimertinib-resistant EGFR-driven Lung Cancer.**: Nineteen patient-derived xenografts from 9 patients with osimertinib-resistant EGFR-mutant NSCLC, with multi-region and temporal sampling. MET pathway activation was a putative resistance mechanism in 6/9 patients with marked spatial/temporal heterogeneity; PDXs with MET amplification by FISH and high phospho-MET (not total c-MET) responded better to osimertinib plus savolitinib than osimertinib alone. Suggests phospho-MET IHC alongside FISH to select patients for MET-directed combinations after osimertinib; limited by small patient numbers and preclinical models only.
  Roper N, El Meskini R, Maity T et al. *Cancer Res Commun*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38276867/
- **Ningetinib plus gefitinib in EGFR-mutant non-small-cell lung cancer with MET and AXL dysregulations: A phase 1b clinical trial and biomarker analysis.**: Phase 1b dose-escalation/expansion trial (n=108) of the MET/AXL inhibitor ningetinib (30-60 mg) plus gefitinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, with MET/AXL status by FISH and IHC. ORR was 30.8% with MET focal amplification, 24.1% with MET overexpression, 0% with MET polysomy and 27.6% with AXL overexpression; the small subgroup with concurrent MET amplification and AXL overexpression (6.8%) had ORR 80% but median PFS only 4.7 months. Clinical evidence that MET-directed combinations after EGFR-TKI resistance need careful biomarker definition; limited by the early-phase single-arm design, first-generation TKI backbone and very small biomarker subgroups.
  Zhao S, Ma Y, Liu L et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38181454/
- **A Phase II trial of alternating osimertinib and gefitinib therapy in advanced EGFR-T790M positive non-small cell lung cancer: OSCILLATE.**: Single-arm phase II trial (n = 47) testing whether alternating osimertinib and gefitinib delays osimertinib resistance in EGFR T790M-positive advanced NSCLC. The 12-month PFS rate was 38% (95% CI 27.5-55), missing the prespecified primary endpoint; serial ctDNA showed T790M loss at progression in 73% and no C797S emergence. It is a negative result for a clonal-evolution-based dosing strategy, and the ctDNA data inform resistance patterns; limitations are the single-arm design and small sample.
  Tan L, Brown C, Mersiades A et al. *Nat Commun*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38418463/
- **Efficacy and safety of intrathecal pemetrexed for TKI-failed leptomeningeal metastases from EGFR+ NSCLC: an expanded, single-arm, phase II clinical trial.**: Prospective single-arm phase II study (n = 132) of intrathecal pemetrexed for leptomeningeal metastases from EGFR-mutant NSCLC after EGFR-TKI progression. Median OS was 12 months (95% CI 10.4-13.6), RANO response rate 80.3%, and myelosuppression (31.8%) was the most common adverse event. It provides prospective CNS efficacy data in a setting with few options; limitations are the single-arm, single-country design without a comparator.
  Fan C, Jiang Z, Teng C et al. *ESMO Open*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38377785/
- **Translational insights and overall survival in the U31402-A-U102 study of patritumab deruxtecan (HER3-DXd) in EGFR-mutated NSCLC.**: Phase I study of the HER3-directed ADC patritumab deruxtecan 5.6 mg/kg in EGFR-mutant NSCLC after EGFR-TKI (safety n = 102). In 78 patients pretreated with a third-generation TKI and platinum chemotherapy, cORR by central review was 41.0%, median PFS 6.4 months and median OS 16.2 months; grade 3 or higher adverse events occurred in 76.5%, and acquired ERBB3 and TOP1 mutations emerged as candidate resistance mechanisms. It supports HER3-DXd as a post-osimertinib option; limitations are the non-randomised phase I design and high toxicity rate.
  Yu HA, Baik C, Kim DW et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38369013/
- **Amivantamab plus lazertinib versus osimertinib in first-line EGFR-mutant advanced non-small-cell lung cancer with biomarkers of high-risk disease: a secondary analysis from MARIPOSA.**: Secondary biomarker analysis of the phase 3 MARIPOSA trial (amivantamab-lazertinib n = 429 vs osimertinib n = 429; baseline ctDNA NGS in 636). Amivantamab-lazertinib prolonged median PFS in TP53 co-mutated disease (18.2 vs 12.9 months, HR 0.65), detectable baseline ctDNA (20.3 vs 14.8 months, HR 0.68), no ctDNA clearance at cycle 3 (16.5 vs 9.1 months, HR 0.49) and baseline liver metastases (18.2 vs 11.0 months, HR 0.58). It suggests the benefit holds in poor-prognosis subgroups; limitations are the exploratory subgroup design and PFS-only endpoints.
  Felip E, Cho BC, Gutiérrez V et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38942080/
- **Amivantamab plus Lazertinib in Previously Untreated EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC.**: Phase 3 randomised MARIPOSA trial (n = 1074; 2:2:1 amivantamab-lazertinib, osimertinib, lazertinib) in untreated EGFR exon 19 deletion or L858R advanced NSCLC. Median PFS by central review was 23.7 vs 16.6 months with amivantamab-lazertinib vs osimertinib (HR 0.70, 95% CI 0.58-0.85), with similar ORR (86% vs 85%) and longer response duration (25.8 vs 16.8 months); interim OS HR was 0.80 (95% CI 0.61-1.05). It establishes a new first-line option against osimertinib; limitations are immature OS at this analysis, more EGFR-related toxicity and higher discontinuation (10% vs 3%).
  Cho BC, Lu S, Felip E et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38924756/
- **Detecting Small Cell Transformation in Patients with Advanced EGFR Mutant Lung Adenocarcinoma through Epigenomic cfDNA Profiling.**: Epigenomic profiling of 26 lung cancer PDXs (126 libraries) defined differences between EGFR-mutant LUAD and transformed SCLC, which were then tested in cfDNA from 1 mL plasma of patients with or without transformation. A multianalyte classifier (H3K27ac, methylation, chromatin accessibility) distinguished EGFR-mutant LUAD from transformed SCLC with an AUROC of 0.94. It points to noninvasive detection of a resistance mechanism that currently requires biopsy; limitations are the small patient cohort size and lack of prospective validation.
  El Zarif T, Meador CB, Qiu X et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38912901/
- **Neoadjuvant and Adjuvant Treatments for Early Stage Resectable NSCLC: Consensus Recommendations From the International Association for the Study of Lung Cancer.**: An IASLC-commissioned international multidisciplinary panel produced 19 recommendations for resectable stage II-III NSCLC, all but one reaching more than 85% consensus and validated by public voting. Key points include biomarker testing in resectable disease, preference for neoadjuvant chemoimmunotherapy in stage III, equipoise in stage II, and a robust preference for adjuvant targeted therapy in patients with sensitizing EGFR or ALK alterations. It is a consensus guideline relevant to perioperative EGFR management; as expert consensus it partly addresses areas where definitive trial data are still lacking.
  Spicer JD, Cascone T, Wynes MW et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38901648/
- **Subcutaneous Versus Intravenous Amivantamab, Both in Combination With Lazertinib, in Refractory Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Primary Results From the Phase III PALOMA-3 Study.**: Randomised phase III trial (n = 418) of subcutaneous vs intravenous amivantamab, both with lazertinib, after progression on osimertinib and platinum chemotherapy. Subcutaneous dosing met pharmacokinetic noninferiority (Ctrough ratio 1.15 and 1.42; AUC ratio 1.03), with ORR 30% vs 33%, median PFS 6.1 vs 4.3 months, fewer infusion-related reactions (13% vs 66%) and first administration time of about 5 minutes vs 5 hours; OS was longer with subcutaneous dosing (HR 0.62, nominal P = .02). It supports a more practical post-osimertinib amivantamab regimen; the OS finding was an exploratory endpoint and efficacy endpoints were secondary.
  Leighl NB, Akamatsu H, Lim SM et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38857463/
- **Phase II Efficacy and Safety of 80 mg Osimertinib in Patients With Leptomeningeal Metastases Associated With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Mutation-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (BLOSSOM).**: Multicentre single-arm phase II study (n = 73) of standard-dose osimertinib 80 mg in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with leptomeningeal metastases after first- or second-generation TKIs, mostly T790M-negative. Median OS was 15.6 months, LM ORR by central review 51.6% (15.6% complete), LM PFS 11.2 months, and the CSF-to-free-plasma ratio was 22%. It supports 80 mg osimertinib for leptomeningeal disease regardless of T790M; limitations are the single-arm design and a population pretreated with earlier-generation TKIs rather than first-line osimertinib.
  Park S, Baldry R, Jung HA et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38828959/
- **Osimertinib after Chemoradiotherapy in Stage III EGFR-Mutated NSCLC.**: Double-blind placebo-controlled phase 3 LAURA trial (n = 216, 2:1) of osimertinib after chemoradiotherapy in unresectable stage III EGFR-mutant NSCLC. Median PFS was 39.1 vs 5.6 months (HR 0.16, 95% CI 0.10-0.24); interim OS at 20% maturity showed 36-month OS 84% vs 74% (HR 0.81, P = 0.53), with grade 3 or higher adverse events in 35% vs 12% and radiation pneumonitis in 48% vs 38%. It establishes consolidation osimertinib after chemoradiotherapy; OS data are immature.
  Lu S, Kato T, Dong X et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38828946/
- **BL-B01D1, a first-in-class EGFR-HER3 bispecific antibody-drug conjugate, in patients with locally advanced or metastatic solid tumours: a first-in-human, open-label, multicentre, phase 1 study.**: First-in-human dose-escalation and expansion phase 1 trial in seven Chinese hospitals (n = 195; 113 with NSCLC, 13 with SCLC) of BL-B01D1, an EGFR-HER3 bispecific ADC, testing several weekly, day 1/8 and every-3-week schedules. Four dose-limiting toxicities occurred in phase 1a; the efficacy results, including any EGFR-mutant NSCLC subgroup data, are cut off in the available abstract text, so no response numbers are reported here. It introduces a new ADC class relevant to EGFR-mutant and post-TKI disease; limitations are the early-phase, mixed-tumour, single-country design.
  Ma Y, Huang Y, Zhao Y et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38823410/
- **Ivonescimab Plus Chemotherapy in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With EGFR Variant: A Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Double-blind phase 3 HARMONi-A trial (n = 322, 55 sites in China) of ivonescimab (PD-1/VEGF bispecific) or placebo plus pemetrexed-carboplatin after EGFR-TKI progression. At the first interim analysis, median PFS was 7.1 vs 4.8 months (HR 0.46, 95% CI 0.34-0.62), with benefit after third-generation TKIs (HR 0.48) and in brain metastases (HR 0.40); ORR was 50.6% vs 35.4%, and grade 3 or higher adverse events occurred in 61.5% vs 49.1%. It is a positive randomised post-TKI trial; limitations are short follow-up (7.9 months), immature OS and a China-only population.
  HARMONi-A Study Investigators, Fang W, Zhao Y et al. *JAMA*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38820549/
- **EHMT2-mediated transcriptional reprogramming drives neuroendocrine transformation in non-small cell lung cancer.**: Preclinical study building neuroendocrine-transformation models of erlotinib resistance in cell lines and cell-derived xenografts. EHMT2 silenced SFRP1 via promoter methylation, activating WNT/beta-catenin signalling, with similar EHMT2/SFRP1 changes in clinical transformed SCLC samples; selective EHMT2 inhibitors restored erlotinib sensitivity and delayed resistance in mice. It identifies a candidate target for small cell transformation; limitations are the reliance on erlotinib (not osimertinib) models and the lack of clinical testing.
  Yang C, Ma S, Zhang J et al. *Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38814866/
- **Discovery of novel EGFR-PROTACs capable of degradation of multiple EGFR-mutated proteins.**: Medicinal chemistry and preclinical study developing the EGFR degrader IV-3 from a weak lead compound. IV-3 inhibited HCC-827, NCI-H1975 and NCI-H1975-TM cells (IC50 0.009, 0.49 and 3.24 uM) with EGFR degradation (DC50 17.93 nM to 0.63 uM), showed antitumour activity in all xenograft models tested, and spared wild-type EGFR cell lines. It adds in vivo data for EGFR PROTACs as a strategy against acquired EGFR mutations; limitations are the micromolar potency against the triple mutant and the absence of any clinical data.
  Du Y, Shi S, Shu C et al. *Eur J Med Chem*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38759458/
- **Design, synthesis, and antitumor activity evaluation of potent fourth-generation EGFR inhibitors for treatment of Osimertinib resistant non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC).**: Structure-activity study of fourth-generation EGFR inhibitors targeting the osimertinib-resistance C797S triple mutation. Lead compound 52 inhibited EGFR L858R/T790M/C797S with an IC50 of 0.55 nM and Ba/F3 triple-mutant cell proliferation with an IC50 of 43.28 nM, and the authors report good pharmacokinetics and "excellent" in vivo efficacy. It adds a candidate fourth-generation TKI with in vivo data for C797S-mediated resistance; limitations are that the abstract gives no quantitative in vivo results and there are no clinical data.
  Zhang Y, Tong L, Yan F et al. *Bioorg Chem*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38691906/
- **Gefitinib vs Gefitinib Plus Pemetrexed and Carboplatin Chemotherapy in EGFR-Variant Lung Cancer-Long-Term Results of a Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Long-term follow-up report of the randomised trial of gefitinib alone vs gefitinib plus pemetrexed-carboplatin as first-line treatment in EGFR-mutant NSCLC. No abstract was available in the retrieved record, so the updated survival numbers cannot be reported here. It matters as updated survival data for TKI-chemotherapy intensification; the full text should be checked for results and limitations.
  Noronha V, Patil V, Menon N et al. *JAMA Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38662354/
- **Pembrolizumab Plus Chemotherapy for Metastatic NSCLC With Programmed Cell Death Ligand 1 Tumor Proportion Score Less Than 1%: Pooled Analysis of Outcomes After Five Years of Follow-Up**: Exploratory pooled analysis of 442 patients with PD-L1 TPS <1% from the phase III KEYNOTE-189 and KEYNOTE-407 studies (and their Japan/China extensions), median follow-up 60.7 months. Pembrolizumab plus chemotherapy improved OS (HR 0.64, 95% CI 0.51-0.79) and PFS (HR 0.66) vs chemotherapy, with 5-year OS 12.5% vs 9.3%. Relevant as long-term trial data, but the population excluded EGFR/ALK alterations, and the analysis is a post hoc pooled subgroup.
  Gadgeel SM, Rodríguez-Abreu D, Halmos B et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38642841/
- **Overcoming Osimertinib Resistance with AKT Inhibition in EGFRm-Driven Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer with PIK3CA/PTEN Alterations**: On-progression plasma ctDNA from the phase III FLAURA and AURA3 trials showed frequent PIK3CA/AKT/PTEN alterations; CRISPR-engineered cell lines, xenografts and PDX models confirmed these confer osimertinib resistance. Adding the AKT inhibitor capivasertib to osimertinib restored sensitivity and improved antitumour effect in vivo. This gives a mechanistic rationale for AKT co-targeting, but the efficacy evidence is preclinical only.
  Grazini U, Markovets A, Ireland L et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38630555/
- **Nicotinamide in Combination with EGFR-TKIs for the Treatment of Stage IV Lung Adenocarcinoma with EGFR Mutations: A Randomized Double-Blind (Phase IIb) Trial**: Randomised, double-blind phase IIb trial (n=110) of nicotinamide 1 g/day vs placebo added to first-generation EGFR-TKIs. Median PFS was 12.7 vs 10.9 months (P=0.2) and OS 31.0 vs 29.4 months (P=0.2), so the primary endpoint was not met. Subgroup signals in women and never-smokers are exploratory and hypothesis-generating only; the comparator TKIs are no longer first-line standard.
  Oh HJ, Bae SC, Oh IJ et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38593249/
- **Co-occurring EGFR p.E709X Mutation Mediates Primary Resistance to the Third-Generation EGFR-TKIs in EGFR p.G719X-Mutant Patients with Advanced NSCLC**: Screening of 4,228 NGS-tested patients plus a multicentre retrospective cohort of 68 EGFR G719X-mutant patients treated with afatinib (n=37) or third-generation TKIs (n=31), with Ba/F3 validation. With co-occurring E709X (about 30% of G719X cases), third-generation TKIs gave ORR 0% vs 47.6% and mPFS 7.2 vs 14.2 months, while afatinib efficacy was unaffected (ORR 71.4% vs 56.7%). This suggests afatinib may be preferable for G719X/E709X, but subgroups are very small and the data retrospective.
  Pang L, Huang Y, Zhuang W et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38578683/
- **[Chinese Expert Consensus on the Standardized Diagnosis and Treatment of Non‑small Cell Lung Cancer with EGFR Exon 20 Insertion Mutations (2024 Edition)]**: Updated national expert consensus (CSCO NSCLC Committee) covering testing methods, treatment and recent trials for EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC, revised from the 2023 edition to reflect newly approved agents. It is a guideline-type document relevant to practice in China; it is consensus-based, the abstract gives no specific recommendations, and the full text is in Chinese.
  Chinese Society of Clinical Oncology Non-small Cell Lung Cancer Committee. *Zhongguo Fei Ai Za Zhi*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39147702/
- **Lazertinib in EGFR-Variant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With CNS Failure to Prior EGFR Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors: A Nonrandomized Controlled Trial**: Multicentre single-arm phase 2 in South Korea (n=40; 38 evaluable, 12 with leptomeningeal disease) of lazertinib 240 mg after CNS progression on first/second-generation EGFR-TKIs. Intracranial ORR was 55% (80% T790M-positive, 43% T790M-negative), median intracranial PFS 15.8 months, and CSF penetration 46.2%; most AEs were grade 1-2. Supports lazertinib CNS activity, but the trial is small, single-arm and in a setting that has largely moved to first-line osimertinib.
  Hong MH, Choi YJ, Ahn HK et al. *JAMA Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39145962/
- **Gefitinib (an EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitor) plus anlotinib (an multikinase inhibitor) for untreated, EGFR-mutated, advanced non-small cell lung cancer (FL-ALTER): a multicenter phase III trial**: Multicentre randomised phase III trial (n=315) of gefitinib plus anlotinib vs gefitinib plus placebo in treatment-naive EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC. PFS improved significantly (HR 0.64, 95% CI 0.48-0.80, P=0.003), with greater benefit reported in brain metastasis and EGFR-amplified subgroups; grade >=3 TEAEs were 49.7% vs 31.0%. Adds to evidence for VEGF/EGFR dual inhibition, but the backbone is a first-generation TKI rather than osimertinib, and OS is not reported in the abstract.
  Zhou HQ, Zhang YX, Chen G et al. *Signal Transduct Target Ther*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39134529/
- **Adaptive radiotherapy (up to 74 Gy) or standard radiotherapy (66 Gy) for patients with stage III non-small-cell lung cancer, according to [18F]FDG-PET tumour residual uptake at 42 Gy (RTEP7-IFCT-1402): a multicentre, randomised, controlled phase 2 trial**: Multicentre randomised non-comparative phase 2 (RTEP7-IFCT-1402, n=158) of PET-adapted boost up to 74 Gy vs standard 66 Gy with chemoradiotherapy in inoperable stage III NSCLC without EGFR/ALK alterations (per PubMed full abstract). 15-month local control was 77.6% vs 71.2%, with acute grade 3-4 AEs 29% vs 45%. Supports a phase 3 test of PET-guided dose adaptation, but the design is non-comparative and EGFR-mutant patients were excluded.
  Vera P, Thureau S, Le Tinier F et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39134086/
- **Tepotinib plus osimertinib in patients with EGFR-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer with MET amplification following progression on first-line osimertinib (INSIGHT 2): a multicentre, open-label, phase 2 trial**: Open-label phase 2 across 179 centres in 17 countries (128 treated; primary population 98 with central FISH MET amplification). Confirmed ORR was 50.0% (95% CI 39.7-60.3) with tepotinib 500 mg plus osimertinib 80 mg; serious treatment-related AEs occurred in 13% and four deaths were possibly treatment-related. A key post-osimertinib, MET-directed option, but single-arm without a randomised comparator and PFS/OS not reported in the abstract.
  Wu YL, Guarneri V, Voon PJ et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39089305/
- **Befotertinib for patients with pretreated EGFR T790M mutated locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC: Final overall survival results from a phase 2 trial**: Single-arm phase 2 (NCT03861156) with cohort A (50 mg, n=176) and cohort B (75-100 mg, n=290). Median OS was 23.9 months (cohort A) and 31.5 months (cohort B), lower in patients with brain metastases (18.6 and 23.0 months); grade >=3 TEAEs were 38.1% and 50.3%. Updated survival data for a new third-generation TKI, but single-arm, second-line T790M setting, and cross-trial comparisons claimed by the authors are unreliable.
  Lu S, Zhang Y, Zhang G et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39089004/
- **Ivonescimab: First Approval**: Drug profile summarising ivonescimab, a PD-1/VEGF-A bispecific antibody, and its first approval in China in combination with pemetrexed and carboplatin for EGFR-mutated advanced non-squamous NSCLC progressing after EGFR-TKI. A regulatory milestone for a new post-TKI option. This is a summary article, not primary data; the approval is China-only and the abstract gives no efficacy numbers.
  Dhillon S. *Drugs*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39073550/
- **Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors With and Without Up-Front Stereotactic Radiosurgery for Brain Metastases From EGFR and ALK Oncogene-Driven Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (TURBO-NSCLC)**: Retrospective study from seven US academic centres (n=317; 200 TKI alone, 117 TKI plus upfront SRS; 79% received osimertinib). Upfront SRS improved time to CNS progression (HR 0.63) and local CNS control (HR 0.30) on multivariable analysis, with no OS difference (41 vs 40 months); benefit appeared largest for metastases >=1 cm. Informs CNS management, but it is retrospective and subject to selection bias, so it needs prospective confirmation.
  Pike LRG, Miao E, Boe LA et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39047224/
- **Longitudinal Analyses of Circulating Tumor DNA for the Detection of EGFR Mutation-Positive Advanced NSCLC Progression During Treatment: Data From FLAURA and AURA3**: Retrospective exploratory analysis of longitudinal EGFR-mutation ctDNA in patients with RECIST progression in the phase III FLAURA (n=146) and AURA3 (n=146) trials. ctDNA progression preceded or co-occurred with RECIST progression in 64% and 56% respectively, with median lead time 1.5-3.4 months. Supports ctDNA monitoring strategies, but the analysis was limited to patients who progressed and had serial samples, and did not test whether acting on ctDNA progression improves outcomes.
  Gray JE, Markovets A, Reungwetwattana T et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39029876/
- **Neoadjuvant Osimertinib for the Treatment of Stage I-IIIA Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: A Phase II Multicenter Study**: Multicentre phase II (n=27) of up to two cycles of neoadjuvant osimertinib before surgery. Major pathological response was 14.8% (95% CI 4.2-33.7) with no pCR, so the primary endpoint was not met; ORR was 52%, median DFS 40.9 months, and 89% proceeded to surgery without AE-related delays. Relevant to perioperative EGFR strategies (e.g. NeoADAURA), but the trial is small and single-arm.
  Blakely CM, Urisman A, Gubens MA et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39028931/
- **Central nervous system efficacy of aumolertinib versus gefitinib in patients with untreated, EGFR-mutated, advanced non-small cell lung cancer: data from a randomized phase III trial (AENEAS)**: Subgroup analysis of the randomised double-blind phase III AENEAS trial: 106 of 429 patients had baseline CNS metastases by blinded neuroradiological review. Aumolertinib prolonged CNS PFS vs gefitinib (29.0 vs 8.3 months; HR 0.31, 95% CI 0.17-0.56), with CNS ORR 85.7% vs 75.0% in those with target lesions. Supports aumolertinib CNS efficacy, but this is a subgroup analysis vs a first-generation comparator, and OS is not reported.
  Lu S, Dong X, Jian H et al. *Cancer Commun (Lond)*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39016053/
- **Capmatinib plus nazartinib in patients with EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer**: Phase 1b/2 (NCT02335944; phase 2 n=144) of capmatinib 400 mg bid plus nazartinib 100 mg qd. ORR was 28.8% in EGFR-TKI-resistant patients (group 1) and 61.7% in treatment-naive patients; in resistant patients ORR was 45.8% if MET-positive vs 26.2% if MET-negative. Supports MET-plus-EGFR co-inhibition, but groups were small, non-randomised and mostly after first/second-generation TKIs, and peripheral oedema occurred in 54.9%.
  Felip E, Metro G, Soo RA et al. *Eur J Cancer*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38986421/
- **Transferrin receptor targeting chimeras for membrane protein degradation**: Preclinical development of transferrin receptor targeting chimeras (TransTACs), heterobispecific antibodies that co-internalise a target membrane protein with TfR1 for lysosomal degradation. TransTACs degraded EGFR, PD-L1, CD20 and CAR proteins, and targeted drug-resistant EGFR ex19del/T790M/C797S lung cancer in a mouse xenograft model. A new degrader modality for C797S-mediated resistance, but the evidence is preclinical only and no efficacy numbers are given in the abstract.
  Zhang D, Duque-Jimenez J, Facchinetti F et al. *Nature*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39322661/
- **Osimertinib after definitive chemoradiotherapy in unresectable stage III epidermal growth factor receptor-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer: analyses of central nervous system efficacy and distant progression from the phase III LAURA study.**: Secondary analysis of the randomised (2:1), placebo-controlled phase III LAURA trial (n=216) of osimertinib after chemoradiotherapy in unresectable stage III EGFRm NSCLC. Osimertinib reduced CNS progression (CNS PFS HR 0.17, 95% CI 0.09-0.32; 12-month CNS progression 9% vs 36%) and time to death or distant metastasis (HR 0.21; 12-month distant metastasis 11% vs 37%). This supports osimertinib consolidation as standard in this setting, including for CNS protection. These were secondary endpoints in a modest-sized trial, and OS data are immature.
  Lu S, Ahn MJ, Reungwetwattana T et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39289145/
- **High-Dose Furmonertinib in Patients With EGFR-Mutated NSCLC and Leptomeningeal Metastases: A Prospective Real-World Study.**: Prospective single-arm real-world study of furmonertinib 240 mg daily (alone or combined) in 48 patients with EGFRm NSCLC and leptomeningeal metastases, 73% previously treated with a third-generation TKI. Median OS was 8.4 months, LM ORR 50% and DCR 92% (RANO-LM), with 6% grade 3 adverse events; CSF cfDNA methylation changes tracked response. It is relevant as a CNS option after osimertinib, but it is small, uncontrolled and allowed combination treatments.
  Chen H, Yang S, Wang L et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39260521/
- **ctDNA Dynamics and Mechanisms of Acquired Resistance in Patients Treated with Osimertinib with or without Bevacizumab from the Randomized Phase II ETOP-BOOSTER Trial.**: Serial plasma NGS (Guardant360) in 136 of 155 patients from the randomised phase II BOOSTER trial of osimertinib with or without bevacizumab after acquired T790M. Week-9 T790M clearance was associated with better PFS on osimertinib (P=0.0097), baseline TP53 and L858R with worse PFS, and acquired C797S was found in 22% (combination) and 13% (osimertinib) at progression; current/former smokers without baseline T790M seemed to benefit from the combination. It adds ctDNA response and resistance data in the second-line osimertinib setting. These are exploratory analyses with small subgroups, and only 42% had samples at progression.
  Soo RA, Dafni U, Han JY et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39250635/
- **A bispecific antibody targeting EGFR and AXL delays resistance to osimertinib.**: Preclinical study in EGFR-mutant lung cancer models comparing anti-AXL antibody (mAb654) and TKI (bemcentinib) combinations with osimertinib, then building an EGFR x AXL bispecific antibody. No osimertinib plus anti-AXL pair prevented relapse; triplets with cetuximab were initially effective, with the mAb654 triplet better on longer follow-up, and the bispecific plus osimertinib consistently inhibited tumour relapses. It is a new bispecific approach to delay osimertinib resistance. The data are from animal models only (no numbers in the abstract), and the authors say clinical trials are needed.
  Simoni-Nieves A, Lindzen M, Giri S et al. *Cell Rep Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39216477/
- **[Chinese Medical Association guideline for clinical diagnosis and treatment of lung cancer (2024 edition)].**: Expert consensus guideline from the Chinese Medical Association updating the 2023 edition across screening, pathology, molecular testing, surgery and treatment. Changes include combined RNA and DNA driver testing, pathology assessment after neoadjuvant therapy, sublobar surgery criteria aligned with the 9th TNM edition, and more treatment options for immunoadjuvant use, EGFR-TKI resistance, EGFR exon 20 insertions, ALK fusions and MET exon 14 skipping. It is a national standard of care in a country with a high share of EGFR-mutant disease. Recommendations follow Chinese drug approvals, the full text is in Chinese, and the abstract gives no specific regimens or evidence grades.
  Oncology Society of Chinese Medical Association. *Zhonghua Yi Xue Za Zhi*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39193605/
- **Phase III KEYNOTE-789 Study of Pemetrexed and Platinum With or Without Pembrolizumab for Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitor-Resistant, EGFR-Mutant, Metastatic Nonsquamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.**: Randomised, double-blind phase III trial (n=492) of pemetrexed-platinum with pembrolizumab or placebo in EGFR-mutant (ex19del/L858R) nonsquamous NSCLC that progressed on EGFR-TKIs. Median PFS was 5.6 vs 5.5 months (HR 0.80, 95% CI 0.65-0.97; P=0.0122, missing the 0.0117 boundary) and median OS 15.9 vs 14.7 months (HR 0.84, 95% CI 0.69-1.02), with grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs in 43.7% vs 38.6%. This negative trial argues against adding PD-1 blockade alone to chemotherapy after TKI failure. The abstract does not break results down by prior TKI (e.g. osimertinib), which limits how well it maps to current first-line practice.
  Yang JC, Lee DH, Lee JS et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39173098/
- **Efficacy and safety of immune checkpoint inhibitors for individuals with advanced EGFR-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer who progressed on EGFR tyrosine-kinase inhibitors: a systematic review, meta-analysis, and network meta-analysis.**: Systematic review with single-arm, pairwise and network meta-analysis of 32 trials (15 RCTs, 17 single-arm; 2886 participants) of ICI-based strategies after EGFR-TKI progression. Compared with chemotherapy, ICI monotherapy shortened PFS (HR 1.73), whereas ICI plus chemotherapy (HR 0.77) and ICI plus antiangiogenic plus chemotherapy (HR 0.54) prolonged it; in the network analysis the four-drug approach beat ICI-chemo (HR 0.71). It is the most complete synthesis to date for choosing post-TKI immunotherapy regimens. The primary outcome was PFS, it mixes single-arm and randomised data, and OS results are not shown in the abstract.
  Zhao Y, He Y, Wang W et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39159630/
- **BLU-945, a potent and selective next-generation EGFR TKI, has antitumor activity in models of osimertinib-resistant non-small-cell lung cancer.**: Preclinical and early clinical report on BLU-945, a reversible fourth-generation EGFR TKI that targets activating and resistance mutations including C797S while sparing wild-type EGFR, tested in cell lines, patient-derived cells/organoids, osimertinib-resistant PDX models and three patients from the phase I/II SYMPHONY trial. BLU-945 shrank tumours in L858R/C797S and triple-mutant (ex19del or L858R/T790M/C797S) PDX models, alone and with osimertinib, and produced tumour shrinkage in the three reported patients. It addresses the lack of targeted options for C797S-driven resistance after osimertinib. Clinical evidence is limited to three selected cases, with no response rates or durability.
  Lim SM, Schalm SS, Lee EJ et al. *Ther Adv Med Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39444426/
- **Treatment Patterns and Clinical Outcomes in Patients With EGFR-Mutated Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer After Progression on Osimertinib.**: Retrospective study in a US nationwide EHR-derived database of 2373 patients on first-line osimertinib, 538 with second-line data; second-line regimens varied widely (65% chemotherapy, 44% EGFR-TKI, 37% ICI). Among 333 patients (PS 0-2) on second-line chemotherapy, continuing osimertinib with chemotherapy was associated with longer PFS (median 10.1 vs 5.9 months; HR 0.48) and OS (17.0 vs 12.8 months; HR 0.64), most clearly in exon 19 deletion. This is real-world evidence on a common post-osimertinib question. It is observational and open to selection bias, so it does not replace randomised data.
  Robinson ND, Canavan ME, Zhan PL et al. *Clin Lung Cancer*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39462746/
- **Small cell transformation in EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer: DLL3 expression and efficacy of immune checkpoint inhibitors or tyrosine kinase inhibitors combined with chemotherapy.**: Retrospective study from 19 European and US centres of 47 patients with EGFR+ NSCLC and small cell transformation, treated with platinum-etoposide alone (n=17), with ICI (n=20) or with an EGFR inhibitor (n=10), plus DLL3 immunohistochemistry. Median PFS was similar across groups (4-6 months), median OS was 13 months with ICI plus chemo vs 10 months with the other options, and DLL3 positivity rose from 0% before to 93% after transformation. This points to ICI-chemo and DLL3-targeted agents as options to test after transformation. Groups are small and non-randomised, and the OS differences are not shown as significant.
  Saalfeld FC, Möller J, Christopoulos P et al. *Eur J Cancer*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39423775/
- **Overcoming tyrosine kinase inhibitor resistance in lung cancer brain metastasis with CTLA4 blockade.**: Single-cell RNA sequencing of surgically resected lung cancer brain metastases with different genotypes and TKI histories, followed by syngeneic mouse brain-metastasis models with TKI-sensitive or TKI-resistant EGFR mutations. TKI treatment raised CTLA4 on T cells via tumour-derived HMGB1, creating an immunosuppressive environment, and CTLA4 blockade plus TKI worked better than TKI alone or TKI plus PD-1 blockade. It suggests a CNS-specific combination strategy against TKI resistance. Evidence is preclinical (no efficacy numbers in the abstract), and the toxicity of TKI plus anti-CTLA4 in patients is untested.
  Fu M, Zhao J, Zhang L et al. *Cancer Cell*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39423817/
- **Final Analysis Data and Exploratory Biomarker Analysis of a Randomized Phase 2 Study of Osimertinib Plus Bevacizumab Versus Osimertinib Monotherapy for Untreated Patients With Nonsquamous NSCLC Harboring EGFR Mutations: The WJOG9717L Study.**: Final survival and exploratory tissue/plasma deep-sequencing analysis of the randomised, open-label phase 2 WJOG9717L trial of first-line osimertinib with or without bevacizumab in EGFR-mutant nonsquamous NSCLC. BICR PFS was 22.1 vs 20.2 months (HR 0.864, 95% CI 0.549-1.359), 3-year OS 57.1% vs 65.0% (HR 1.271), and there was no PFS benefit in patients with plasma TP53 mutations (n=42; HR 1.107). Together with RAMOSE, it helps define which anti-VEGF combinations with osimertinib are worth pursuing. It is a phase 2 trial not powered for OS, and biomarker subgroups are small.
  Kenmotsu H, Sakai K, Mori K et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39399795/
- **Safety and efficacy of consolidative stereotactic radiotherapy for oligo-residual EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer after first-line third-generation EGFR-tyrosine kinase inhibitors: a single-arm, phase 2 trial.**: Single-arm phase II trial at two Chinese centres (n=61) of consolidative stereotactic radiotherapy, given without stopping the TKI, for oligo-residual disease after first-line third-generation EGFR-TKIs. Median PFS was 29.9 months (80% CI 22.4-32.4), meeting the primary endpoint; grade 3 or higher toxicity was 6.6%, and a propensity-matched comparison with TKI alone gave PFS HR 0.46. It supports prospective testing of local consolidation in the osimertinib era. It is non-randomised, uses 80% CIs, has short follow-up (21.1 months) and immature OS.
  Zhou Y, Peng L, Liang F et al. *EClinicalMedicine*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39398493/
- **Benmelstobart plus anlotinib in patients with EGFR-positive advanced NSCLC after failure of EGFR TKIs therapy: a phase I/II study.**: Phase I/II ALTER-L038 study of the anti-PD-L1 antibody benmelstobart plus the anti-angiogenic TKI anlotinib, without chemotherapy, after EGFR-TKI failure; 55 patients were in the phase II expansion. Median PFS was 9.0 months, median OS 28.9 months, ORR 25.5%, DCR 87.3% and median duration of response 19.8 months, with grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs in 25.5%. It is a chemotherapy-free post-TKI option worth randomised testing. It is single-arm, Chinese-only, and the abstract does not separate patients by prior osimertinib.
  Shi M, Chen P, Cui B et al. *Signal Transduct Target Ther*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39389963/
- **First-line zorifertinib for EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer with central nervous system metastases: The phase 3 EVEREST trial.**: Randomised phase 3 trial (n=439) of the brain-penetrant EGFR-TKI zorifertinib vs gefitinib or erlotinib in untreated EGFR-mutant NSCLC with non-irradiated CNS metastases. BICR PFS was 9.6 vs 6.9 months (HR 0.719; p=0.0024) and intracranial PFS was longer (BICR HR 0.467); OS is immature (HR 0.833 in patients who later got third-generation TKIs). It adds a CNS-active first-line option. The comparator was a first-generation TKI rather than osimertinib, which limits relevance to current practice; the trial was industry-funded.
  Zhou Q, Yu Y, Xing L et al. *Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39389055/
- **A Multicenter Open-Label Randomized Phase II Study of Osimertinib With and Without Ramucirumab in Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitor-Naive EGFR-Mutant Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (RAMOSE trial).**: Randomised (2:1) open-label phase II trial of first-line osimertinib with or without ramucirumab in EGFR-mutant metastatic NSCLC (139 evaluable patients), reported at a preplanned interim analysis. Median PFS was 24.8 vs 15.6 months (HR 0.55, 95% CI 0.32-0.93; p=0.023), with no difference in ORR and grade 3 TRAEs in 53% vs 41%. It is one of the few positive osimertinib-plus-anti-VEGF trials and contrasts with negative bevacizumab data. It is an interim analysis with 57 PFS events, short follow-up (16.6 months), and no OS data yet.
  Le X, Patel JD, Shum E et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39378386/
- **Thoracic Radiotherapy Improves the Survival in Patients With EGFR-Mutated Oligo-Organ Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Treated With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors: A Multicenter, Randomized, Controlled, Phase III Trial.**: Multicentre randomised phase III trial (NROG-002, n=118) of first-line EGFR-TKI with or without concurrent thoracic radiotherapy (60 Gy to primary and involved nodes) in EGFR-mutant oligo-organ metastatic NSCLC. TKI plus radiotherapy improved PFS (17.1 vs 10.6 months; HR 0.57) and OS (34.4 vs 26.2 months; HR 0.62), with more severe TRAEs (11.9% vs 5.1%). It is randomised phase III evidence for local consolidation in oligometastatic EGFRm disease. The trial is small, ran from 2016, and the abstract does not say which TKI generation was used, so the benefit on top of osimertinib is unproven.
  Sun H, Li M, Huang W et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39374473/
- **RELAY: Final Overall Survival for Erlotinib Plus Ramucirumab or Placebo in Untreated, EGFR-Mutated Metastatic NSCLC**: Final OS analysis of the global double-blind phase 3 RELAY trial (n=449, ex19del/L858R, no CNS metastases) of erlotinib plus ramucirumab vs erlotinib plus placebo. Despite the earlier PFS benefit (HR 0.59), OS was not improved (HR 0.98, 95% CI 0.78-1.24; median 51.1 vs 46.0 months) after 45.1 months median follow-up, with only non-significant directional trends in L858R and TP53-co-mutated subsets. This closes the question of OS benefit for adding VEGFR2 blockade to a first-generation TKI; limitations are that OS was a secondary endpoint, >50% of patients received subsequent osimertinib, and erlotinib is no longer the standard backbone.
  Nakagawa K, Garon EB, Seto T et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39622410/
New trial registrations:

#### Trial registrations and posted results

- **A Study of HS-20117 Combined With Aumolertinib in Participants With Advanced Non-Squamous Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer** (NCT06417008): Phase 2/3, sponsor Hansoh BioMedical R&D, recruiting, planned n=1080. Tests HS-20117, a fully human EGFR-MET bispecific antibody, combined with aumolertinib in ex19del/L858R advanced non-squamous NSCLC (record lists HS-20117 + aumolertinib; comparator not specified in the record). Registration only, no results.
  Hansoh BioMedical R&D Company. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06417008
- **JMT101 in Combination With Osimertinib Versus Osimertinib Alone as First-Line Treatment for EGFR-Sensitive Non-Squamous NSCLC** (NCT06735391): Phase 3, randomised, open-label, positive-controlled, sponsor Shanghai JMT-Bio, recruiting, planned n=516. Arms: JMT101 + osimertinib vs osimertinib alone in newly diagnosed advanced EGFR-sensitising NSCLC. Registration only, no results.
  Shanghai JMT-Bio Inc. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06735391
- **Phase III Study of AK112 for NSCLC Patients (HARMONi)** (NCT06396065): Phase 3, randomised, double-blind, sponsor Summit Therapeutics, active not recruiting, n=420. Arms: AK112 (ivonescimab) + pemetrexed/carboplatin vs placebo + pemetrexed/carboplatin in EGFR-mutant non-squamous NSCLC progressing on or after EGFR-TKI. Registration record only, no results.
  Summit Therapeutics. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06396065
- **A Study of SKB264 in Combination With Osimertinib Versus Osimertinib in EGFR-Mutant Non-Squamous NSCLC** (NCT06670196): Phase 3, sponsor Sichuan Kelun-Biotech, active not recruiting, n=420. Arms: SKB264 (sacituzumab tirumotecan) + osimertinib vs osimertinib as first-line treatment. Registration only, no results.
  Sichuan Kelun-Biotech Biopharmaceutical Co., Ltd. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06670196
- **Sacituzumab Tirumotecan (MK-2870) Versus Pemetrexed and Carboplatin in EGFR-Mutated NSCLC After EGFR-TKI (MK-2870-009)** (NCT06305754): Phase 3, sponsor Merck Sharp & Dohme, active not recruiting, n=550. Arms: sacituzumab tirumotecan vs pemetrexed + carboplatin after progression on EGFR-TKIs; primary hypothesis includes superior OS. Registration only, no results.
  Merck Sharp & Dohme LLC. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06305754
- **First-line Osimertinib With or Without Datopotamab Deruxtecan for EGFRm NSCLC (TROPION-Lung14)** (NCT06350097): Phase 3, open-label, sponsor AstraZeneca, recruiting, planned n=582. Arms: osimertinib + Dato-DXd vs osimertinib monotherapy in first-line ex19del/L858R NSCLC; event-driven with ~8-year duration. Registration only, no results.
  AstraZeneca. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06350097
- **A Study of SHR-A2009 Versus Platinum-based Chemotherapy in EGFR-mutated, Advanced or Metastatic NSCLC** (NCT06671379): Phase 3, randomised, open-label, sponsor Suzhou Suncadia Biopharmaceuticals, active not recruiting, n=498. Arms: SHR-A2009 monotherapy vs platinum-based doublet in patients who failed EGFR-TKI. Registration only, no results.
  Suzhou Suncadia Biopharmaceuticals Co., Ltd. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06671379
- **Adjuvant Target Therapy Guided by ctDNA-MRD in EGFR-mutant II-IIIA NSCLC (ECTOP-1022)** (NCT06323148): Phase 3, sponsor Fudan University, not yet recruiting, planned n=226. Post-resection ctDNA-negative patients are randomised 1:1 to observation vs adjuvant osimertinib to test non-inferiority of de-escalation; ctDNA-positive patients receive standard treatment. Registration only, no results.
  Fudan University. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06323148
- **A Study Comparing BL-B01D1 With Platinum Based Chemotherapy in NSCLC (PANKU-Lung01)** (NCT06382116): Phase 3, randomised, open-label, sponsor Sichuan Baili Pharmaceutical, active not recruiting, n=432. Arms: BL-B01D1 vs pemetrexed + cisplatin/carboplatin in EGFR-sensitising non-squamous NSCLC after EGFR-TKI failure. Registration only, no results.
  Sichuan Baili Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06382116
- **Dato-DXd With or Without Osimertinib Compared With Platinum-Based Doublet Chemotherapy in EGFR-Mutated NSCLC (TROPION-Lung15)** (NCT06417814): Phase 3, sponsor AstraZeneca, recruiting, planned n=744. Arms: Dato-DXd + osimertinib, Dato-DXd monotherapy, or platinum-pemetrexed; primary endpoint PFS. Registration only, no results.
  AstraZeneca. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06417814
Results posted on ClinicalTrials.gov (the record gives no numbers):
- **KEYNOTE-789: Pemetrexed + Platinum With or Without Pembrolizumab in TKI-Resistant EGFR-Mutated Non-squamous NSCLC** (NCT03515837): Phase 3, sponsor Merck Sharp & Dohme, completed, n=492. Arms: pembrolizumab vs saline placebo, each with pemetrexed + platinum, after EGFR-TKI failure (including osimertinib); primary endpoints PFS and OS. Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.

Single regimen of tepotinib + osimertinib for MET-amplified resistance after first-line osimertinib. Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.
