- **FLAURA2: Osimertinib With or Without Chemotherapy as First-Line Treatment in EGFR-Mutated NSCLC** (NCT04035486): Phase 3, sponsor AstraZeneca, active not recruiting, n=587. Arms: osimertinib + pemetrexed/platinum vs osimertinib monotherapy. Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.
  AstraZeneca. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04035486
- **LASER301: Lazertinib as First-Line Treatment in EGFR-Mutation-Positive Advanced NSCLC** (NCT04248829): Phase 3, double-blind, sponsor Yuhan, completed, n=393. Arms: lazertinib vs gefitinib (double-dummy). Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.
  Yuhan Corporation. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04248829
- **PAPILLON: Amivantamab Plus Carboplatin-Pemetrexed vs Carboplatin-Pemetrexed in EGFR Exon 20 Insertion NSCLC** (NCT04538664): Phase 3, sponsor Janssen, active not recruiting, n=308. Arms: amivantamab + carboplatin-pemetrexed vs carboplatin-pemetrexed; primary endpoint PFS. Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.
  Janssen Research & Development, LLC. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04538664
- **MARIPOSA: Amivantamab and Lazertinib Versus Osimertinib in EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC** (NCT04487080): Phase 3, sponsor Janssen, active not recruiting, n=1074. Arms: amivantamab + lazertinib vs osimertinib (plus a lazertinib arm with placebo) in ex19del/L858R first line. Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.
  Janssen Research & Development, LLC. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04487080
- **HERTHENA-Lung01: Patritumab Deruxtecan in EGFR-mutated NSCLC** (NCT04619004): Phase 2, sponsor Daiichi Sankyo, active not recruiting, n=277. Patritumab deruxtecan at fixed dose vs up-titration after at least one EGFR-TKI and platinum chemotherapy. Results were posted; no figures are in the monitored record.
  Daiichi Sankyo. *ClinicalTrials.gov*, 2024. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04619004

### nsclc-resistance-nextgen

- **Trastuzumab deruxtecan in patients with metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer (DESTINY-Lung01): primary results of the HER2-overexpressing cohorts from a single-arm, phase 2 trial.**: Primary analysis of the HER2-overexpressing (IHC 3+/2+, no HER2 mutation) cohorts of the multicentre single-arm phase 2 DESTINY-Lung01: trastuzumab deruxtecan 6.4 mg/kg (cohort 1, n=49) or 5.4 mg/kg (cohort 1A, n=41) in pretreated non-squamous NSCLC. Confirmed ORR by central review was 26.5% (95% CI 15.0-41.1; 13/49, all partial) in cohort 1; the abstract supplied here is truncated, so the cohort 1A result and safety data are not reported. Defines activity of a HER2 ADC in HER2-overexpressing NSCLC, a relevant option when HER2 is upregulated; single-arm, small cohorts, and short follow-up (about 11-12 months).
  Smit EF, Felip E, Uprety D et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38547891/
- **Efficacy and Safety of Taletrectinib in Chinese Patients With ROS1+ Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Phase II TRUST-I Study**: Pivotal single-arm phase II study of the CNS-active ROS1 TKI taletrectinib in 173 Chinese patients (106 TKI-naive, 67 crizotinib-pretreated). Confirmed ORR was 91% (intracranial 88%) in TKI-naive and 52% (intracranial 73%) in crizotinib-pretreated patients, with median PFS 7.6 months after crizotinib and 8 of 12 G2032R-mutant tumours responding. Relevant as a post-TKI option with activity against the main ROS1 resistance mutation; limitations are single-arm design, a Chinese-only population, short follow-up in the pretreated cohort, and frequent (mostly low-grade) transaminase rises and diarrhoea.
  Li W, Xiong A, Yang N et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38822758/
- **Datopotamab Deruxtecan Versus Docetaxel for Previously Treated Advanced or Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Randomized, Open-Label Phase III TROPION-Lung01 Study.**: Global open-label phase III RCT (n=604) of the TROP2 ADC Dato-DXd 6 mg/kg vs docetaxel in pretreated advanced NSCLC. PFS improved (median 4.4 vs 3.7 months; HR 0.75, P=.004), but OS did not differ significantly (12.9 vs 11.8 months; HR 0.94); benefit was confined to nonsquamous histology (PFS HR 0.63), with a trend against Dato-DXd in squamous disease. Grade >=3 treatment-related AEs were fewer (25.6% vs 42.1%), but drug-related ILD/pneumonitis was more common (8.8% vs 4.1%). Main limitations: open-label design and no significant OS benefit; the abstract gives no separate result for the EGFR-mutant subgroup.
  Ahn MJ, Tanaka K, Paz-Ares L et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39250535/

### lung-top-journals

- **Perioperative Toripalimab Plus Chemotherapy for Patients With Resectable Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Neotorch Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Randomised, placebo-controlled phase 3 trial at 50 Chinese hospitals (n=501; interim analysis of 404 stage III patients) of toripalimab or placebo with platinum chemotherapy for 3 cycles pre- and 1 cycle post-surgery, then toripalimab maintenance. EFS was improved (median not estimable vs 15.1 months; HR 0.40, 95% CI 0.28-0.57) and major pathological response was 48.5% vs 8.4%, pCR 24.8% vs 1.0%. Supports perioperative PD-1 blockade in stage III NSCLC but excluded EGFR/ALK-altered nonsquamous tumours, so it does not inform EGFR-mutant disease; follow-up was short (median 18.3 months), OS immature, and 92% of patients were male.
  Lu S, Zhang W, Wu L et al. *JAMA*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38227033/
- **Pembrolizumab With or Without Lenvatinib for First-Line Metastatic NSCLC With Programmed Cell Death-Ligand 1 Tumor Proportion Score of at least 1% (LEAP-007): A Randomized, Double-Blind, Phase 3 Trial.**: Double-blind phase 3 trial (n=623) of lenvatinib or placebo added to first-line pembrolizumab in PD-L1 TPS >=1% metastatic NSCLC without EGFR/ROS1/ALK alterations. At a prespecified futility analysis (median follow-up 15.9 months) OS was not improved (14.1 vs 16.4 months; HR 1.10, 95% CI 0.87-1.39) despite longer PFS (6.6 vs 4.2 months; HR 0.78), and grade 3-5 TRAEs were 57.9% vs 24.4%; the study was unblinded and lenvatinib stopped. A negative trial for VEGFR-TKI plus PD-1 combinations; not applicable to EGFR-mutant disease, and OS is from an interim futility analysis.
  Yang JC, Han B, De La Mora Jiménez E et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38159809/
- **Telisotuzumab Vedotin Monotherapy in Patients With Previously Treated c-Met Protein-Overexpressing Advanced Nonsquamous EGFR-Wildtype Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer in the Phase II LUMINOSITY Trial.**: Two-stage phase II trial of the c-Met-directed ADC telisotuzumab vedotin 1.9 mg/kg every 2 weeks in previously treated, c-Met protein-overexpressing nonsquamous EGFR-wildtype NSCLC (n = 172). ORR by central review was 28.6% (34.6% in c-Met-high, 22.9% in c-Met-intermediate), median response duration 8.3 months, median PFS 5.7 months and median OS 14.5 months; peripheral sensory neuropathy (30%) was the most common treatment-related adverse event. It is a JCO trial result for a new ADC in NSCLC, but it enrolled EGFR-wildtype patients only, so it does not directly address the post-EGFR-TKI setting; it is also single-arm with no comparator.
  Camidge DR, Bar J, Horinouchi H et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38843488/
- **Plinabulin plus docetaxel versus docetaxel in patients with non-small-cell lung cancer after disease progression on platinum-based regimen (DUBLIN-3): a phase 3, international, multicentre, single-blind, parallel group, randomised controlled trial.**: Phase 3 RCT (n=559, 87% Asian) in EGFR wild-type NSCLC after platinum-based therapy, comparing docetaxel plus plinabulin vs docetaxel plus placebo. Median OS was 10.5 vs 9.4 months (stratified HR 0.82, 95% CI 0.68-0.99; p=0.04), with more grade 3-4 diarrhoea (9% vs 1%) and transient grade 3 hypertension (18% vs 3%). It is a rare positive second-line trial, but the OS gain is about one month, the trial ran before routine first-line immunotherapy for many patients, and it excludes EGFR-mutant disease.
  Han B, Feinstein T, Shi Y et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39265599/
- **Zongertinib (BI 1810631), an Irreversible HER2 TKI, Spares EGFR Signaling and Improves Therapeutic Response in Preclinical Models and Patients with HER2-Driven Cancers.**: Discovery and preclinical characterisation of zongertinib, a covalent HER2-selective TKI that spares wild-type EGFR, tested in cell lines (including T-DXd-resistant cells), NSCLC xenografts and early patients. It showed potent antitumour activity in HER2-dependent NSCLC xenografts, enhanced ADC and KRAS G12C inhibitor activity without obvious toxicity, and produced objective responses in individual patients with HER2-driven cholangiocarcinoma and breast cancer. It matters because sparing wild-type EGFR addresses the dose-limiting toxicity of pan-ERBB TKIs in HER2-mutant NSCLC. The clinical evidence here is anecdotal and from non-lung tumours; NSCLC efficacy needs the ongoing trials.
  Wilding B, Woelflingseder L, Baum A et al. *Cancer Discov*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39248702/
- **Durvalumab With or Without Tremelimumab in Combination With Chemotherapy in First-Line Metastatic NSCLC: Five-Year Overall Survival Outcomes From the Phase 3 POSEIDON Trial.**: Prespecified long-term OS analysis (median follow-up 63.4 months) of the 3-arm phase 3 POSEIDON trial (n=1013) in EGFR/ALK wild-type metastatic NSCLC. Tremelimumab plus durvalumab and chemotherapy kept its OS benefit over chemotherapy (HR 0.76, 95% CI 0.64-0.89; 5-year OS 15.7% vs 6.8%), while durvalumab plus chemotherapy gave HR 0.84 (95% CI 0.72-1.00); the benefit was greater in nonsquamous disease and seen across PD-L1 levels and STK11/KEAP1/KRAS-mutant subgroups. These data confirm durable benefit, but subgroup findings are exploratory and the trial excluded EGFR-mutant disease.
  Peters S, Cho BC, Luft AV et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39243945/
- **Durvalumab Versus Chemotherapy as First-line Treatment for Metastatic NSCLC With Tumor PD-L1 Expression of 25% or Higher: Results From the Randomized Phase 3 PEARL Study.**: Final analysis of the open-label phase 3 PEARL trial (n=669) of first-line durvalumab monotherapy vs chemotherapy in EGFR/ALK wild-type metastatic NSCLC with PD-L1 TC of 25% or higher. OS was numerically better with durvalumab (HR 0.84, 95% CI 0.71-0.99; median 14.6 vs 12.8 months; p=0.037, not significant at the prespecified level), with no difference in the low-early-mortality population (HR 0.96), and grade 3-4 treatment-related AEs were 15.5% vs 45.9%. It is a negative trial for durvalumab monotherapy, consistent with other single-agent ICI data. It excludes EGFR-mutant disease and was open-label.
  Lu S, Wu L, Wang Q et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39521433/
- **Capmatinib in MET exon 14-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer: final results from the open-label, phase 2 GEOMETRY mono-1 trial.**: Final analysis of the non-randomised, multi-cohort phase 2 GEOMETRY mono-1 trial of capmatinib in MET-dysregulated, EGFR wild-type NSCLC (373 treated; 160 with METex14). In METex14 disease, ORR was 68% (41/60) treatment-naive and 44% (44/100) previously treated, with median follow-up of 46-67 months; peripheral oedema (47%) was the most common TRAE, and grade 3-4 serious AEs occurred in 44%. These mature data confirm capmatinib as a standard targeted option for METex14 NSCLC. It is single-arm with no comparator, and EGFR-mutant patients were excluded, so it does not address MET-driven resistance after EGFR-TKIs.
  Wolf J, Hochmair M, Han JY et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39362249/
- **LIBELULE: A Randomized Phase III Study to Evaluate the Clinical Relevance of Early Liquid Biopsy in Patients With Suspicious Metastatic Lung Cancer.**: Randomised phase III trial (n=319) adding ctDNA testing (InVisionFirst-Lung) at the first visit vs standard diagnostic work-up in patients with suspected advanced lung cancer. The primary endpoint, time to appropriate treatment, was not significantly shortened (29.0 vs 34 days, p=0.26), although time to genomic results fell (17.9 vs 25.6 days) and sensitivity analyses showed shorter time to treatment in patients with first-line targetable alterations (21 vs 37.4 days). This matters for fast identification of EGFR and other drivers, but the trial was negative on its primary endpoint and the subgroup findings are exploratory.
  Swalduz A, Schiffler C, Curcio H et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39694415/
- **Final Overall Survival and Long-Term Safety of Lorlatinib in Patients With ALK-Positive NSCLC From the Pivotal Phase 2 Study: A Brief Report.**: Final analysis (minimum 5 years' follow-up) of the global single-arm phase 2 lorlatinib study in ALK-positive NSCLC, in expansion cohorts defined by prior therapy. Median OS was not reached in treatment-naive patients (n=30), and was 37.4 months after one second-generation ALK TKI (n=28) and 19.2 months after two or more ALK TKIs (n=111). Adverse events led to dose reduction in 28% and permanent discontinuation in 13%. It provides long-term data on sequencing after earlier TKIs in ALK disease, but there is no comparator arm and some cohorts are small.
  Ou SI, Solomon BJ, Besse B et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39581380/
- **Perioperative tislelizumab plus neoadjuvant chemotherapy for patients with resectable non-small-cell lung cancer (RATIONALE-315): an interim analysis of a randomised clinical trial.**: Double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 3 trial at 50 Chinese sites (n=453, stage II-IIIA). At interim analysis (median follow-up 22 months), perioperative tislelizumab improved event-free survival (HR 0.56, 95% CI 0.40-0.79) and major pathological response (56% vs 15%). Serious treatment-related AEs were more frequent (15% vs 8%). This adds another positive perioperative immunotherapy trial, but EFS is immature, OS was not yet reported, 91% of patients were male, and all were enrolled in China.
  Yue D, Wang W, Liu H et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39581197/
- **Systemic Therapy for Small Cell Lung Cancer: ASCO Guideline Rapid Recommendation Update.**: ASCO rapid update of its SCLC systemic therapy guideline in response to new practice-changing data, based on an evidence review that follows ASCO methodology. The abstract does not state the revised recommendations, so the specific changes must be checked in the full text.
  Kalemkerian GP, Khurshid H, Ismaila N et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39565968/
- **Toripalimab Plus Chemotherapy as a First-Line Therapy for Extensive-Stage Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Phase 3 EXTENTORCH Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Double-blind phase 3 RCT at 49 Chinese sites (n=442) of toripalimab or placebo plus etoposide-platinum in first-line ES-SCLC. Toripalimab improved PFS (HR 0.67) and OS (HR 0.80, P=.03; median 14.6 vs 13.3 months), and grade >=3 AEs were similar (about 90% in both arms). Exploratory whole-exome biomarkers were reported. The absolute OS gain is modest, follow-up was relatively short (median 13.7 months), and the trial was China-only.
  Cheng Y, Zhang W, Wu L et al. *JAMA Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39541202/
- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2024.2.**: Scheduled update of ASCO's living guideline on systemic therapy for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations, including EGFR, written by a standing expert panel that reviews the literature continuously. The abstract does not list the updated recommendations, so the EGFR-relevant changes must be read in the full text or on the online guideline page.
  Bazhenova L, Ismaila N, Abu Rous F et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39531596/
- **The Phase 3 KEYLYNK-006 Study of Pembrolizumab Plus Olaparib Versus Pembrolizumab Plus Pemetrexed as Maintenance Therapy for Metastatic Nonsquamous NSCLC.**: Phase 3 RCT (n=672 randomised after chemo-pembrolizumab induction) in nonsquamous NSCLC without targetable alterations. Olaparib maintenance did not improve PFS (7.1 vs 8.3 months; HR 1.12) or OS (20.7 vs 23.0 months; HR 1.04) compared with pemetrexed maintenance. This is a clearly negative result for adding PARP inhibition to maintenance; it does not apply to driver-positive (e.g. EGFR) disease, which was excluded.
  Gray JE, Schenker M, Sendur MAN et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39521434/
- **Nivolumab plus ipilimumab versus carboplatin-based doublet as first-line treatment for patients with advanced non-small-cell lung cancer aged ≥70 years or with an ECOG performance status of 2 (GFPC 08-2015 ENERGY): a randomised, open-label, phase 3 study.**: Open-label French phase 3 RCT (n=216; median age 74) of nivolumab plus ipilimumab vs carboplatin doublet in older or PS2 patients with stage IV NSCLC and no known oncogenic alteration. The trial was stopped early for futility at a preplanned interim analysis (33% of events); median OS was 14.7 vs 9.9 months in the abstract excerpt available (the remaining results were truncated). The study addresses an under-represented population, but early termination and the open-label design limit conclusions.
  Lena H, Greillier L, Cropet C et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39486424/
- **Pembrolizumab With or Without Maintenance Olaparib for Metastatic Squamous NSCLC That Responded to First-Line Pembrolizumab Plus Chemotherapy.**: Double-blind phase 3 RCT (KEYLYNK-008, n=591 randomised) in squamous NSCLC with disease control after chemo-pembrolizumab induction. Olaparib numerically improved PFS (8.3 vs 5.4 months; HR 0.77, p=0.004) but missed the prespecified significance boundary, and OS was unchanged (19.1 vs 18.6 months; HR 1.01), with more treatment-related AEs (76.5% vs 65.1%). Together with KEYLYNK-006, this argues against PARP maintenance with pembrolizumab.
  Hochmair M, Schenker M, Cobo Dols M et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39477187/
- **Perioperative chemotherapy and nivolumab in non-small-cell lung cancer (NADIM): 5-year clinical outcomes from a multicentre, single-arm, phase 2 trial.**: Final report of the Spanish single-arm phase 2 NADIM trial (n=46, resectable stage IIIA) of neoadjuvant chemo-nivolumab followed by 1 year of adjuvant nivolumab. At 5 years, PFS was 65.0% and OS 69.3%; grade >=3 TRAEs occurred in 30% during neoadjuvant and 19% during adjuvant treatment. These long-term data support perioperative immunotherapy, but the trial is small, single-arm and used AJCC 7th edition staging.
  Provencio M, Nadal E, Insa A et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39419061/
- **CTLA4 blockade abrogates KEAP1/STK11-related resistance to PD-(L)1 inhibitors.**: Analysis of the randomised phase III POSEIDON trial combined with genetic screens and mouse models of Kras-driven NSCLC. Patients with STK11 and/or KEAP1 mutations benefited from adding tremelimumab to durvalumab-chemotherapy but not from durvalumab alone; Keap1 loss was the strongest predictor of dual ICB efficacy in mice, with a mechanism involving CD4+ effector cells and myeloid reprogramming. This points to dual ICB for STK11/KEAP1-altered NSCLC, but the clinical findings come from a retrospective biomarker analysis, and the abstract gives no effect sizes.
  Skoulidis F, Araujo HA, Do MT et al. *Nature*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39385035/
- **Stereotactic vs Hypofractionated Radiotherapy for Inoperable Stage I Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The LUSTRE Phase 3 Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Canadian phase 3 RCT (n=233 of 324 planned; 2:1) of SBRT vs hypofractionated conventional RT (60 Gy/15) in medically inoperable stage I NSCLC, including central tumours. Three-year local control did not differ significantly (87.6% vs 81.2%; HR 0.61, P=.15), nor did EFS or OS. Late grade 3-4 toxicity with SBRT was 11% for central and 1.8% for peripheral tumours, with one possible grade 5 event in an ultracentral lesion. The trial under-accrued against its target, so it had limited power to detect a difference.
  Swaminath A, Parpia S, Wierzbicki M et al. *JAMA Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39298144/
- **Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab plus chemotherapy followed by adjuvant pembrolizumab compared with neoadjuvant chemotherapy alone in patients with early-stage non-small-cell lung cancer (KEYNOTE-671): a randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled, phase 3 trial.**: Second interim analysis of the global phase 3 KEYNOTE-671 trial (n=797, resectable stage II-IIIB N2). Perioperative pembrolizumab improved OS (36-month 71% vs 64%; HR 0.72, 95% CI 0.56-0.93) and EFS (median 47.2 vs 18.3 months; HR 0.59). Grade 3-5 treatment-related AEs occurred in 45% vs 38%. This makes it the first perioperative immunotherapy trial to show an OS benefit; the abstract excerpt does not report results by driver-mutation subgroup.
  Spicer JD, Garassino MC, Wakelee H et al. *Lancet*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39288781/
- **Durvalumab after Chemoradiotherapy in Limited-Stage Small-Cell Lung Cancer.**: Double-blind phase 3 ADRIATIC trial (durvalumab n=264 vs placebo n=266, plus a still-blinded durvalumab-tremelimumab arm) of consolidation after concurrent chemoradiotherapy in LS-SCLC. Durvalumab improved OS (median 55.9 vs 33.4 months; HR 0.73) and PFS (16.6 vs 9.2 months; HR 0.76), with similar grade 3-4 AEs (24.4% vs 24.2%). This is a practice-changing first-interim result; the durvalumab-tremelimumab comparison is not yet reported.
  Cheng Y, Spigel DR, Cho BC et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39268857/
- **Telehealth vs In-Person Early Palliative Care for Patients With Advanced Lung Cancer: A Multisite Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Randomised equivalence trial at 22 US centres (n=1,250 patients with advanced NSCLC, 548 caregivers) of video vs in-person early palliative care. Quality of life at week 24 was equivalent (FACT-L 99.7 vs 97.7; difference 2.0 within a +/-4 margin), with no differences in mood, coping or satisfaction, although caregivers took part less often by video (36.6% vs 49.7%). This supports telehealth to widen access; the population was 82.7% White and the trial was US-only.
  Greer JA, Temel JS, El-Jawahri A et al. *JAMA*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39259563/
- **Comparison of platinum combination chemotherapy plus pembrolizumab versus platinum combination chemotherapy plus nivolumab-ipilimumab for treatment-naive advanced non-small-cell lung cancer in Japan (JCOG2007): an open-label, multicentre, randomised, phase 3 trial.**: Open-label Japanese phase 3 RCT (n=295, driver-negative) of chemo plus nivolumab-ipilimumab vs chemo plus pembrolizumab. Accrual was stopped early because of treatment-related deaths (7% in the nivolumab-ipilimumab arm); OS did not differ (median 23.7 vs 20.5 months; HR 0.98), and grade >=3 non-haematological AEs were more common (60% vs 41%). This indicates an unfavourable benefit-risk for adding CTLA-4 blockade, but the trial was underpowered (117 of 329 planned deaths).
  Shiraishi Y, Nomura S, Sugawara S et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39159638/
- **High-dose hyperfractionated simultaneous integrated boost radiotherapy versus standard-dose radiotherapy for limited-stage small-cell lung cancer in China: a multicentre, open-label, randomised, phase 3 trial.**: Open-label phase 3 RCT at 16 Chinese hospitals (n=224, LS-SCLC) of twice-daily thoracic RT with a 54 Gy simultaneous integrated boost vs 45 Gy. Median OS was longer with 54 Gy (60.7 vs 39.5 months; HR 0.55, p=0.003), and toxicities were similar (grade 3-4 oesophagitis 13% vs 12%). This could change the dose standard, but the trial was moderate-sized, open-label, limited to patients aged 18-70 years and run only in China; it predates immunotherapy consolidation.
  Yu J, Jiang L, Zhao L et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39146944/
- **Management of Stage III Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: ASCO Guideline Rapid Recommendation Update.**: ASCO rapid update of its stage III NSCLC guideline, issued because of new practice-changing data and based on an evidence review under ASCO methodology. The abstract does not state the revised recommendations (for example, any on consolidation targeted therapy for EGFR-mutant disease), so these must be checked in the full text.
  Daly ME, Singh N, Ismaila N et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39042842/
- **Benmelstobart, anlotinib and chemotherapy in extensive-stage small-cell lung cancer: a randomized phase 3 trial.**: Double-blind three-arm phase 3 ETER701 trial (n=738) in treatment-naive ES-SCLC. Benmelstobart (PD-L1) plus anlotinib plus etoposide-carboplatin improved OS vs chemotherapy alone (median 19.3 vs 11.9 months; HR 0.61), whereas anlotinib plus chemotherapy alone did not reach significance (HR 0.86). Grade >=3 treatment-related AEs were very common (93.1% vs 87.0%). The OS result is from an interim analysis, and the trial did not include an immunochemotherapy-alone arm, so the specific contribution of anti-angiogenesis is not isolated.
  Cheng Y, Chen J, Zhang W et al. *Nat Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38992123/
- **Circulating Tumor DNA-Guided De-Escalation Targeted Therapy for Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: A Nonrandomized Controlled Trial.**: Single-centre prospective nonrandomised trial (n=60) of stopping TKIs in driver-positive advanced NSCLC with no radiological disease after TKI plus local consolidative therapy, restarting on progression, detectable ctDNA or rising CEA. Median PFS was 18.4 months, with a median treatment break of 9.1 months; 14 patients stayed off TKI (median 20.3 months), and the retreatment response rate was 96%. This is an early test of ctDNA-guided de-escalation relevant to EGFR TKIs, but the trial was small, single-centre and nonrandomised, follow-up was short (19.2 months) and OS is immature.
  Dong S, Wang Z, Zhang JT et al. *JAMA Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38869865/
- **Sacituzumab Govitecan Versus Docetaxel for Previously Treated Advanced or Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Randomized, Open-Label Phase III EVOKE-01 Study.**: Open-label phase III RCT (n=603) of the TROP2 ADC sacituzumab govitecan vs docetaxel after platinum, anti-PD-(L)1 and, where relevant, targeted therapy. The primary OS endpoint was not met (median 11.1 vs 9.8 months; HR 0.84, one-sided P=.0534), and PFS was similar (4.1 vs 3.9 months). OS appeared better in patients not responding to their last anti-PD-(L)1 regimen (HR 0.75, exploratory), and fewer patients discontinued for toxicity (6.8% vs 14.2%). It is a second negative OS result for TROP2 ADCs vs docetaxel; the abstract gives no data for the actionable-alteration subgroup.
  Paz-Ares LG, Juan-Vidal O, Mountzios GS et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38843511/
- **Recommendations for the use of next-generation sequencing (NGS) for patients with advanced cancer in 2024: a report from the ESMO Precision Medicine Working Group.**: Updated ESMO Precision Medicine Working Group consensus on routine tumour NGS, based on ESCAT actionability with cost and access considered. Tumour NGS remains recommended in advanced non-squamous NSCLC, and the recommendation is extended to breast cancer, several rare tumours and tumour-agnostic alterations where matched drugs are accessible. This underpins multiplex testing for EGFR and co-alterations; it is an expert consensus rather than new trial data.
  Mosele MF, Westphalen CB, Stenzinger A et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38834388/
- **Stepped Palliative Care for Patients With Advanced Lung Cancer: A Randomized Clinical Trial.**: Nonblinded noninferiority RCT at 3 US academic centres (n=507, advanced lung cancer) of stepped palliative care (visits at key points, stepped up if QoL fell) vs monthly early palliative care. Stepped care halved visits (2.4 vs 4.7 by week 24) with noninferior QoL (FACT-L 100.6 vs 97.8), but noninferiority was not shown for days in hospice (19.5 vs 34.6). This offers a less resource-intensive model; it was run only at academic centres and 84.6% of patients were White.
  Temel JS, Jackson VA, El-Jawahri A et al. *JAMA*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38824442/
- **Lorlatinib Versus Crizotinib in Patients With Advanced ALK-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: 5-Year Outcomes From the Phase III CROWN Study.**: Five-year post hoc update of the phase III CROWN RCT (n=296) of first-line lorlatinib vs crizotinib in ALK-positive NSCLC. Median PFS was not reached vs 9.1 months (HR 0.19; 5-year PFS 60% vs 8%), and time to intracranial progression was markedly longer (HR 0.06); no new ALK resistance mutations were found in end-of-treatment ctDNA. It sets a benchmark for CNS-active first-line TKIs, but the analysis is post hoc and investigator-assessed, OS was not reported, and the comparator is a first-generation TKI.
  Solomon BJ, Liu G, Felip E et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38819031/
- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2024.1.**: The 2024.1 update of ASCO's living guideline for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations, including EGFR, produced by a standing panel with continuous literature review. The abstract does not list the changed recommendations, so the full text or online guideline page should be consulted (it was superseded later in 2024 by version 2024.2).
  Owen DH, Ismaila N, Freeman-Daily J et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38815183/
- **Perioperative Nivolumab in Resectable Lung Cancer.**: Double-blind phase 3 CheckMate 77T trial in resectable stage IIA-IIIB NSCLC comparing neoadjuvant nivolumab-chemotherapy followed by 1 year of adjuvant nivolumab vs chemotherapy-placebo. At the interim analysis (median follow-up 25.4 months), 18-month EFS was 70.2% vs 50.0% (HR 0.58), and pCR was 25.3% vs 4.7%; grade 3-4 TRAEs occurred in 32.5% vs 25.2%. It supports a perioperative nivolumab approach, but OS is immature and the design cannot isolate the contribution of the adjuvant phase.
  Cascone T, Awad MM, Spicer JD et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38749033/
- **RESILIENT Part 2: A Randomized, Open-Label Phase III Study of Liposomal Irinotecan Versus Topotecan in Adults With Relapsed Small Cell Lung Cancer.**: Open-label phase III RCT (n=461) of second-line liposomal irinotecan vs topotecan in relapsed SCLC. OS did not differ (7.9 vs 8.3 months; HR 1.11), and PFS was similar, although ORR was higher (44.1% vs 21.6%) and grade >=3 related AEs were fewer (42.0% vs 83.4%). The primary endpoint was negative, so this does not change the second-line standard; the open-label design limits the AE comparison.
  Spigel DR, Dowlati A, Chen Y et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38648575/
- **Alectinib in Resected ALK-Positive Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.**: Global open-label phase 3 ALINA trial (n=257) of 2 years of adjuvant alectinib vs platinum chemotherapy after resection of stage IB-IIIA (AJCC 7th) ALK-positive NSCLC. Two-year DFS was 93.8% vs 63.0% in stage II-IIIA (HR 0.24) and 93.6% vs 63.7% in the ITT population, and CNS DFS also improved (HR 0.22). This is a practice-changing adjuvant targeted-therapy result, analogous to ADAURA in EGFR disease, but OS was immature, the trial was open-label, and follow-up was short.
  Wu YL, Dziadziuszko R, Ahn JS et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38598794/
- **CONTACT-01: A Randomized Phase III Trial of Atezolizumab + Cabozantinib Versus Docetaxel for Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer After a Checkpoint Inhibitor and Chemotherapy.**: Open-label phase III RCT (n=366) of atezolizumab plus cabozantinib vs docetaxel after progression on anti-PD-(L)1 and platinum chemotherapy. OS was not improved (10.7 vs 10.5 months; HR 0.88), despite a modest PFS gain (4.6 vs 4.0 months; HR 0.74), and grade 5 AEs occurred in 7.6% vs 6.0%. This adds to the negative trials of IO rechallenge combinations after immunotherapy; follow-up was relatively short (minimum 10.9 months).
  Neal J, Pavlakis N, Kim SW et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38552197/
- **Tislelizumab Plus Platinum and Etoposide Versus Placebo Plus Platinum and Etoposide as First-Line Treatment for Extensive-Stage SCLC (RATIONALE-312): A Multicenter, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Randomized, Phase 3 Clinical Trial.**: Double-blind phase 3 RCT in China (n=457) of tislelizumab or placebo with etoposide-platinum in first-line ES-SCLC. OS improved (median 15.5 vs 13.5 months; HR 0.75), as did PFS (4.7 vs 4.3 months; HR 0.64); grade >=3 TRAEs were 86% in both arms. This confirms a class effect of PD-(L)1 added to chemotherapy in ES-SCLC, but the absolute gains are modest, follow-up was short (median 14.2 months) and the trial was China-only.
  Cheng Y, Fan Y, Zhao Y et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38460751/
- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2023.3.**: ASCO living-guideline update for driver-positive stage IV NSCLC, including EGFR, based on a systematic review of RCTs published February-October 2023 that identified 8 new trials; recommendations were updated by driver alteration and line of therapy. The abstract does not state the specific EGFR changes, and versions 2024.1 and 2024.2 have since superseded it.
  Jaiyesimi IA, Leighl NB, Ismaila N et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38417091/
- **First-line penpulimab combined with paclitaxel and carboplatin for metastatic squamous non-small-cell lung cancer in China (AK105-302): a multicentre, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 3 clinical trial.**: Double-blind phase 3 RCT at 74 Chinese hospitals (n=350) of the PD-1 antibody penpulimab or placebo with paclitaxel-carboplatin in first-line advanced squamous NSCLC. The abstract reports improved IRRC-assessed PFS at the final analysis (median follow-up 24.7 months); the effect sizes are cut off in the available excerpt. Generalisability is limited: 93% of patients were male, 99% Han Chinese, and PFS rather than OS was the primary endpoint.
  Zhong H, Sun S, Chen J et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38309287/
- **Iruplinalkib (WX-0593) Versus Crizotinib in ALK TKI-Naive Locally Advanced or Metastatic ALK-Positive NSCLC: Interim Analysis of a Randomized, Open-Label, Phase 3 Study (INSPIRE).**: Open-label phase 3 RCT (n=292) of the new-generation ALK TKI iruplinalkib vs crizotinib in ALK TKI-naive NSCLC. At interim analysis, IRC-assessed PFS was longer (27.7 vs 14.6 months; HR 0.34), and intracranial ORR was 90.9% vs 60.0%, although only 11 and 15 patients had measurable CNS disease. Grade 3-4 TRAEs occurred in 51.7% with iruplinalkib. The comparator is crizotinib rather than a current standard (alectinib, lorlatinib), and OS is immature.
  Shi Y, Chen J, Yang R et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38280448/
- **Repotrectinib in ROS1 Fusion-Positive Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.**: Registrational single-arm phase 1-2 TRIDENT-1 trial of the next-generation ROS1 TKI repotrectinib. Response rates were 79% in ROS1 TKI-naive patients (n=71; median PFS 35.7 months) and 38% after one prior ROS1 TKI (n=56; median PFS 9.0 months), with responses in 10 of 17 patients carrying the G2032R resistance mutation; dizziness (58%) and dysgeusia (50%) were common, but only 3% discontinued. This provides a post-TKI option with activity against on-target resistance; it is a single-arm trial without a comparator.
  Drilon A, Camidge DR, Lin JJ et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38197815/
- **A global phase 3 study of serplulimab plus chemotherapy as first-line treatment for advanced squamous non-small-cell lung cancer (ASTRUM-004).**: Global phase 3 RCT (n=537; 2:1) of serplulimab or placebo with nab-paclitaxel-carboplatin in first-line squamous NSCLC without targetable alterations. PFS improved (HR 0.53), and the final analysis showed improved OS (HR 0.73, p=0.010), with similar grade >=3 drug-related AEs (35.2% vs 32.4%). It adds another PD-1 option for squamous disease; median survival times and the regional make-up of the population are not given in the abstract.
  Zhou C, Hu Y, Arkania E et al. *Cancer Cell*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38181795/
